### Page 25

14 October 2026
25
For Europe, the central challenge is not simply to accelerate AI adoption, but to turn widespread experimentation
into productive, inclusive and trusted use Curiosity is not the main constraint: Europeans have embraced AI almost as
widely as Americans, but the technology has made fewer inroads into the workplace, and workers are less prepared to
use it effectively. The priority is therefore to remove the barriers that prevent AI from translating into productivity gains

and broader economic benefits. This requires action on five fronts:
1. Move AI from private experimentation into productive workplace use.  General-purpose technologies like
AI generate productivity gains only after firms make large complementary investments. These investments
show up as costs today, dragging down earnings and productivity in the short run, the so-called productivity
J-curve. Because the costs are immediate while the benefits are delayed, private investment may fall short,

in particular for SMEs, which face disproportionately high costs for the same implementation effort⁸. Europe
should reduce these costs that are holding back implementation, especially for SMEs, through accelerated
depreciation or temporary tax credits for identifiable complementary investments such as software integration,
data infrastructure, cybersecurity and process redesign. Public support should be tied to actual deployment
in business processes and, where possible, measurable productivity or efficiency gains rather than generic AI

subscriptions.
2. Close the training gap by starting with employers. This is primarily a provision problem: many workers
receive no offer of AI training, while most of those offered it take it up or plan to do so. Employer-led training
should therefore be supported through training tax credits, public co-financing and individual learning accounts,
with a focus on occupation-specific skills and groups currently least served including women, mid-career workers,

the secondary-educated and employees in smaller firms. Europe already has useful instruments: Germany’s
“Qualifizierungschancengesetz” subsidizes training costs and, in some cases, wage costs for employees affected
by technological change, while the EU  encourages member states to develop individual learning accounts that
give workers portable training entitlements. Where firms receive public support for major AI investments, this
could be linked to a workforce-training plan.

3. Shift labor-market policy from protecting jobs to supporting transitions.  Because AI is likely to change tasks
and occupations more extensively than it eliminates jobs outright, governments need to identify adjustment
pressures before they translate into unemployment. Combining occupation-level AI-exposure indicators
with vacancy, wage and skill-demand data can help identify vulnerable workers and emerging employment
opportunities. Workers in exposed occupations should have access to retraining while still employed, stronger

job-matching services, mobility grants and temporary wage insurance where moving into a new role initially
means lower pay. Such measures must go beyond traditional active labor-market programs, whose short-term
effects are often limited and vary considerably across program types and participants. Better targeting is crucial:
Policy priorities:
adopting AI better, not just faster
⁸  For productivity discussion, see: Brynjolfsson, Rock & Syverson 2021. „The Productivity J-Curve: How Intangibles Complement General Purpose

Technologies,“ American Economic Journal: Macroeconomics. For SMEs discussion, see: OECD 2025, “ AI adoption by small and medium-sized enter -
prises”,

### Page 26

Allianz Research
26
programs that invest in human capital accumulation, such as classroom-based vocational training and on-the-
job traineeships, show large gains, in particular for the long-term unemployed, whose skills have eroded during
extended periods out of work, and for women⁹.  Recent evidence confirms that well-designed interventions
do indeed deliver: cost-benefit analyses show that such training schemes can be highly cost effective, and

programs combining part-time work placements with training and short-term community work placements for
the unemployed produce lasting earnings gains for participants¹⁰. The key lesson is therefore to replace broad,
expensive programs with measures that are tightly linked to real-time labor-market signals.
4. Make sure the productivity dividend is broadly shared. AI is likely to raise productivity but could also
shift national income from labor to capital, and if automation outruns capital accumulation, wages could fall

even while output rises¹¹. Policy should therefore address both labor income and household wealth. On the
income side, broader access to skills can help translate productivity gains into better employment prospects
and wages. . On the asset side, funded pensions, employee share plans and wider household capital-market
participation can help more people benefit directly from AI-driven returns, particularly in Europe, where  equity
ownership is heavily concentrated among wealthier households¹². At the same time, the tax system should

avoid unnecessarily favoring capital over labor and adapt if a larger share of income shifts toward capital and
economic rents. Europe is particularly exposed here, as its tax systems rely more heavily on labor income taxes
and social contributions than the US, potentially putting greater pressure on public finances if the labor share of
income declines¹³.
5. Strengthen trust by targeting the risks people actually identify. Misinformation, data misuse and labor-

and human oversight and appeal rights for high-impact AI decisions. Rules should be credible and proportionate:
safeguards that build trust can support adoption, while complex or inconsistent requirements can slow diffusion,
especially for smaller firms. EU-wide templates, sector-specific guidance and regulatory sandboxes can help
make implementation more predictable without weakening protections.

“Impact Evaluation of Ireland’s Active Labour Market Policies ”,

The survey queried more than 8,000 respondents across eight countries: Austria, France, Germany, Italy, Poland, Spain,
the United Kingdom and the United States. It was conducted as a web-based survey between 22 April and 25 May 2026.
Overall responsibility lies with Allianz Research, Allianz Investment Management SE. Sample planning and fieldwork

were carried out by Qualtrics.
Respondents were selected using representative quota sampling. Qualtrics was given quotas specifying how many
people to survey in each country and which selection criteria to apply, with quotas set in line with official statistics for
gender and age groups. A comparison with official statistics confirms that the resulting sample broadly corresponds to
the population aged 18 and over in each of the eight countries.

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: searching for signal
By Sian Fenner, Ryan Wells & Illiana Jain
13:33 October 02 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia, this week’s main event was the RBA Monetary Policy Board’s (MPB) decision to raise the
cash rate by 25bps to 4.60%. This outcome was universally expected given Governor Bullock had
already communicated that upside risks to inflation had materialised – the conditionality established by

the MPB back in August for considering another rate hike.

### Page 2

Governor Bullock made explicit note of three upside risks. The Middle East conflict was emphasised as
the risk that had “materialised”, given the re-escalation and renewed surge in crude oil and refined
product prices over recent months. The RBA’s liaison warns this could lead to further pass-through of
costs and more persistent inflation pressures. On top of this, domestic capacity pressures and the data
centre/AI investment boom were also noted as upside risks that the MPB remain alert to.

In a video update mid-week, Chief Economist Luci Ellis explains why the bar for a follow-up rate hike in
November is low. We now expect a 25bp increase in November, provided there is no sudden resolution
of the Middle East conflict beforehand, or some other event that significantly lowers the outlook for
energy-related costs.
Subsequently, the August CPI revealed that underlying (trimmed mean) inflation rose 0.2% (3.4%yr),
which was slightly below the market’s expectation but spot on Westpac’s forecast. As usual, the

monthly detail was mixed, but the key drivers of underlying inflation remain centred on housing-related
components, particularly rents and new dwelling purchases. Headline inflation remained volatile, with
the unwind of the fuel excise cut causing another spike in fuel prices. We have maintained our forecast
for a 0.9% lift in trimmed mean inflation in Q3, and with upside risks building for Q4, a follow-up rate
hike in November appears most likely.

Regarding the current growth pulse, household spending surprised with a flat reading in August.
Services spend fell back following recent strength that was driven by major sporting events, but this
was offset by an increase in goods spend, particularly in fuel and electric vehicles. Together with our
latest card data for early September, the consumer spending pulse is shading a touch softer. However,
another solid outcome for business investment could once again be on the cards for Q3. August’s

goods trade data showcased another significant spike in data centre-related equipment imports, up
$4.3bn, similar in scale to Q1. While clearly import-driven, our modelling suggests that the local
economic impact of such investment is likely to be around half of the total investment.
Before moving offshore, a final note on housing. Cotality’s latest update revealed that dwelling prices
fell another 1.1% nationally in September. While Sydney and Melbourne have led the correction to

date, the fact that the other capitals are also reporting price declines clearly indicates that the
correction is deepening and broadening, as higher interest rates collide with the tax policy changes
announced in May’s budget. Sentiment effects indeed remain at play, as evinced by the continued pull-
back in investor credit growth. Westpac anticipates a peak-to-trough decline of around 7%, with tight
on-market supply limiting the extent of the correction. For more, see our latest Housing Pulse.

Offshore, there was plenty of data to dissect.
In the US, inflation eased in August despite ongoing strength in household demand and higher energy
prices. Headline PCE inflation rose 0.3%mth and 3.4%yr, while core PCE increased 0.2%mth and
3.0%yr. Encouragingly for the FOMC, the share of PCE components rising by more than 3% over the
past year, a measure frequently cited by Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh, declined to 45% from 49%,
suggesting some easing in broad-based inflation pressures. At the same time, household spending

remained robust, increasing 0.9%mth, equivalent to around 0.6%mth in real terms. That said, personal
income growth was soft, rising 0.2% in the month, signalling households opted to draw down savings.

### Page 3

The ISM manufacturing survey was stronger than the headline suggested. While the index edged down
0.1pt to 54.5, manufacturing has now expanded for nine consecutive months. There were some signs
of capacity constraints emerging, with production softened in the month but backlogs up 4.6pts to 56.4,
suggesting that firms struggled to keep pace with demand. New orders rose 1.6pts to 55.3, primarily
due to domestic demand as new export orders eased, backlogs increased. Firms responded by lifting

employment, 1.5pts to 52.7. Against this backdrop manufacturers saw a renewed acceleration in cost
pressures, with the prices paid index jumping 6.8pts to 77.9. Firms cited higher steel and aluminium
prices, elevated energy costs, rising freight charges and ongoing semiconductor shortages, with every
major industry reporting higher input costs during the month.
The past week’s data will have done nothing to bridge the clear divide across the FOMC, as evident in

this week’s busy Fedspeak. Some participants argued for further rate hikes, citing strong inflation,
diminished supply capacity and policy that is not yet restrictive. Others counselled patience, pointing to
progress on inflation and that September’s hike buys time to assess upcoming data. The one thing
both agree on is that inflation will be the primary determinant of the timing and extent of further rate
hikes. Our view remains that one further hike is likely this cycle to bring policy closer to neutral.

In China, policymakers announced another round of targeted support measures aimed at stabilising
growth and supporting the struggling property sector. The PBOC lowered rates on its Pledged
Supplementary Lending facility by 25bps to 1.5% and expanded quotas for targeted lending programs.
Authorities also introduced subsidies for eligible first-home buyers. The subsidy applies to homes with
a floor area of 120m² or less and a value below CNY1.5mn, with support capped at 1ppt of mortgage

costs, up to CNY10,000 and for a maximum term of five years. The measures complement reforms
announced in August to improve developer financing conditions and support housing completion.
However, weak credit demand and confidence remains a significant constraint, suggesting policy
transmission is still limited.
The September PMI data pointed to a modest improvement in activity, although the details were less
convincing. Manufacturing returned to expansionary territory, rising 0.9pts to 50.1, supported by

stronger production and new orders. Much of the improvement, however, reflected a rebound in prices,
with the raw materials price index rising 7.6pts and producer prices increasing 6.2pts. Labour market
conditions remained weak, with the employment index falling a further 0.6pts and remaining below 50
for almost four years. Conditions in the non-manufacturing sector also improved, with the services PMI
lifting from 49.0 to 50.2, driven largely by stronger construction activity. Input and output prices moved

higher, selling prices returned back above 50, export orders improved to 48.5 and employment
increased 0.5pts, although at 45.9 it remains firmly in contractionary territory. Overall, the surveys
suggest activity is stabilising and policy support is gaining some traction, but weak labour demand,
subdued credit growth and soft domestic demand indicate the recovery remains fragile.
In Japan, the September Tankan survey delivered another constructive signal on the economy and

reinforced the case for further policy normalisation. Business conditions improved by 2pts for large
manufacturers and 5pts more broadly, while profitability indicators strengthened and capital
expenditure intentions remained elevated. Large manufacturers expect fixed investment to increase by
11.6% in FY2026, while large non-manufacturers expect investment growth of 11.2%, pointing to

### Page 4

Related articles
continued confidence in the medium-term outlook.
Capacity indicators improved further across both manufacturing and non-manufacturing sectors, with
measures of excess production capacity becoming less negative and indicating that demand remains
sufficiently strong to keep pressure on existing capacity. Labour shortages also intensified, with
employment conditions becoming more negative across most industries and firm sizes, pointing to a

progressively tighter labour market. Inflation expectations remained anchored around 2%, providing
further evidence that Japan is moving towards a more sustainable inflation environment. Overall, the
survey was consistent with an economy operating close to capacity and supports the view that a
December rate hike remains a realistic possibility.

### Page 1

Doanh thu 2Q2026 tăng trưởng chậm 
nhưng biên lợi nhuận gộp vẫn được 
duy trì do tăng giá bán

Sản lượng tiêu thụ 6M2026 chững lại 
do tâm lý nhập hàng thận trọng và 
hoạt động xây dựng phục hồi chậm

Biên lợi nhuận gộp được dự báo sẽ 
thu hẹp cuối năm, phản ánh chi phí 
nguyên liệu cao hơn trong 1Q2026

Khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP, giá mục 
tiêu 166,100 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Doanh thu/LNST 2Q2026 đạt lần lượt 1,320/367 tỷ VND (+1%/+11% YoY). Lũy kế 
6M2026, doanh thu đạt 2,777 tỷ VND (+3% YoY) trong khi LNST ước đạt 671 tỷ VND 
(+9% YoY). Biên lãi gộp 6M2026 cải thiện đạt 47.0% (+240 bps YoY), chủ yếu do việc 
tăng giá bán giúp doanh nghiệp bù đắp được mức tăng của chi phí đầu vào.

Lũy kế 6M2026, sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP ước đạt 43,920 tấn, giảm 5% svck. Việc 
BMP thu hẹp các chương trình chiết khấu, với mức chiết khấu bình quân khoảng 
5.2% trong 2Q2026, làm giảm động lực nhập hàng của các đại lý và khiến sản lượng 
tiêu thụ chậm lại. Dù vậy, thị phần toàn quốc của BMP ước đạt 28% (+1 đpt svck) 
nhờ các doanh nghiệp khác trong ngành gặp khó khăn khi giá hạt nhựa tăng mạnh.

Biên lợi nhuận gộp của BMP dự kiến thu hẹp trong 2H2026 trước áp lực chi phí 
nguyên liệu đầu vào tăng. Tuy nhiên, chúng tôi kỳ vọng mức độ ảnh hưởng sẽ thấp 
hơn so với các doanh nghiệp trong ngành do (1) các lô nguyên liệu giá cao từ 
1Q2026 đã được đưa vào sản xuất và tiêu thụ một phần trong 1H2026; (2) BMP chủ 
động nâng gấp đôi hàng tồn kho để ứng phó với biến động bất lợi của giá nguyên 
liệu; (3) hơn 90% nhu cầu nguyên liệu uPVC được cung ứng từ các đối tác trong 
nước như hệ sinh thái SCG tại Việt Nam, AGC Chemicals và LSP; và (4) công ty duy 
trì giá bán bình quân ở mức cao.

Dựa trên kết quả kinh doanh và định giá, chúng tôi khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP với cổ 
phiếu BMP, giá mục tiêu là 166,100 VND/cổ phiếu (tương ứng với mức tăng 14.3% 
so với giá đóng cửa ngày 23/09/2026).

Tăng trưởng doanh thu và lợi nhuận thuần 5 năm gần nhất Hoạt động kinh doanh

Nhựa Bình Minh (BMP) là doanh nghiệp hàng đầu 
trong lĩnh vực sản xuất ống nhựa với thị phần 
dẫn đầu tại thị trường nội địa (27% vào cuối 
2023), thị trường chính tập trung tại khu vực 
miền Nam. BMP có 4 nhà máy với tổng công suất 
đạt 150,000 tấn/năm. 
Nguồn: BMP, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Kỳ vọng đà phục hồi của xây dựng dân dụng và bất động sản miền Nam sẽ thúc đẩy sản lượng tiêu 
thụ tăng trưởng trong 2026–2027. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Giá PVC được kỳ vọng sẽ hạ nhiệt và khả năng giữ giá bán đầu ra ở mức cao giúp BMP giữ biên lợi 
nhuận gộp ở nền cao. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo

### Page 4

4

Nhu cầu nhập hàng thận trọng và 
cắt giảm các đợt chiết khấu trong 
kỳ của BMP làm giảm sản lượng tiêu 
thụ trong 2Q2026

Sự hồi phục của nhu cầu xây dựng 
dân dụng và nguồn cung các dự án 
bất động sản tại miền Nam sẽ thúc 
đẩy tăng trưởng sản lượng 2H2026 
 Sản lượng tiêu thụ trong 2Q2026 ước đạt 20,100 tấn, giảm 11% svck, nâng tổng 
sản lượng tiêu thụ 6M2026 ước đạt 43,920 tấn, giảm 5% svck do các yếu tố bất 
lợi từ bên ngoài như (1) giá hạt nhựa tăng cao khiến thị trường dè chừng trong 
việc nhập hàng vì lo ngại nguồn cung bất ổn (2) BMP đã thu hẹp các chương 
trình chiết khấu so với 2025 ngay trước thời điểm xung đột bùng nổ, giúp tỷ lệ 
chi phí bán hàng/doanh thu về mức 11.3%, giảm 800 bps so với 1Q2026 ở mức 
19.3%, nhưng đồng thời cũng khiến sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP suy giảm do 
chính sách bán hàng thắt chặt hơn với các đối thủ có thị phần thấp hơn.

Chúng tôi kỳ vọng sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP dần hồi phục từ 3Q2026 khi nhu 
cầu nhập hàng của các đại lý cải thiện sau giai đoạn thận trọng trong nửa đầu 
năm. Về cuối năm, hoạt động xây dựng dự kiến tăng tốc khi điều kiện thi công 
thuận lợi hơn sau mùa mưa. Bên cạnh đó, việc triển khai các dự án nhà ở tại 
TP.HCM và các tỉnh lân cận được kỳ vọng hỗ trợ nhu cầu ống nhựa trong giai 
đoạn tới. Theo đó, chúng tôi dự phóng sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP lần lượt đạt 
96,695/100,125/96,750 tấn trong 2026/2027/2028.

thu và lợi nhuận thuần/sản lượng tiêu thụ

### Page 5

5

Lợi thế từ hệ sinh thái công ty mẹ 
SCG và đối tác Nhật Bản giúp BMP 
có nhiều dư địa để nâng cao hiệu 
quả hoạt động

Theo chia sẻ từ ban lãnh đạo, BMP có lợi thế vì nhập nguyên liệu từ công ty mẹ 
là hệ sinh thái SCG tại Việt Nam và đối tác Nhật Bản là AGC Chemicals Vietnam, 
giúp giảm được 5% thuế nhập khẩu và chi phí vận chuyển. Điều này giúp BMP 
hưởng lợi kép khi có thể tăng được giá bán theo thị trường nhưng nguyên vật 
liệu rẻ hơn so với các doanh nghiệp trong ngành. KBSV nhận thấy định hướng 
dài hạn của BMP là cân bằng lợi ích cổ đông và khách hàng, vì vậy việc giảm giá 
bán để giành thị phần không phải lựa chọn ưu tiên của doanh nghiệp mà là 
nâng cao tỷ suất lợi nhuận/sản phẩm thành phẩm.

KBSV dự báo biên lợi nhuận gộp thu hẹp trong 2H2026 khi chi phí nguyên liệu 
giá cao từ 1H2026 được phản ánh vào giá vốn, và hồi phục trong 2027–2028 
nhờ đặc tính giữ giá bán ít thay đổi của nhóm nhựa. Chúng tôi kỳ vọng giá PVC 
resin chịu áp lực giảm từ (1) nhu cầu xây dựng tại Trung Quốc còn yếu khi thị 
trường bất động sản chưa hồi phục rõ ràng; và (2) các nhà sản xuất giải phóng 
hàng tồn kho sau mùa mưa, làm tăng sản lượng hạt nhựa PVC resin. Theo đó, 
KBSV dự phóng biên lợi nhuận gộp của BMP lên 46.4%/46.7%/46.5% trong 
2026/2027/2028.

Biểu đồ 6. Giá PVC resin Trung Quốc   Biểu đồ 7. Tương quan giá PVC resin Trung Quốc và biên lãi 
gộp của BMP

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>

ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: October 19 - 23

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD:
October 19 - 23
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 18 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

Last week’s inflation reports came in hot. September’s CPI rose 0.6%
m/m, lifting headline inflation from 3.4% y/y in August to 3.7%. Core CPI
rose 0.3% m/m, taking its y/y rate from 2.4% to 2.6%. Both topped
expectations. PPI final demand jumped 1.3% m/m. That pushed its y/y
rate to 6.3%, the highest since 2022. Retail sales rose 0.8% m/m, though
just 0.2% excluding gas stations.
Odds of a 25bps hike at the October 27-28 FOMC meeting fell from 22%

before the CPI report to 17%, despite the hot inflation data. Fed Chair
Kevin Warsh offered no signal on October's meeting at the IMF and World
Bank annual meetings in Bangkok on Friday. “I don’t prejudge meetings,”
he said. Odds of a December hike rose. We still expect the Fed to reverse
the remaining two of last year’s three 25bps “insurance” cuts. Fed officials
are now in their pre-meeting blackout period.
This week’s data calendar is light, with earnings season taking center

stage. Friday’s flash PMIs are the main US releases. The Fed’s Financial
Stability Report and Canada’s federal budget are tentatively due Tuesday.
Here’s more:
(1) ADP & Unemployment Claims. ADP’s weekly employment reading
(Tue) follows a rise in the four-week average to 24,750 for the week ended
September 26, the highest since early June and up from a late-July
bottom of 8,250 (chart).
Initial jobless claims (Thu) follow last week’s 199,000, with the four-week

average at 198,500 (chart). Continuing claims fell to 1,710,000 for the

### Page 4

week ended October 2, matching their four-week average. The labor
market continues to improve.
(2) Flash PMIs. S&P Global’s flash M-PMI and NM-PMI for October (Fri)
follow September readings of 55.9 and 58.8 (chart). The services prices
component is worth watching after ISM’s NM-PMI prices-paid index
jumped to 70.7 in September, the highest since July 2022.
Flash PMIs for Australia and Japan (Thu) and for the Eurozone, Germany,
France, and the UK (Fri) also land this week. September’s M-PMIs were

52.9 for the Eurozone and 52.1 for the UK.
(3) Earnings. The earnings reporting season picks up this week. Netflix,
GE Aerospace, and Northrop Grumman report on Tuesday. Tesla and
Meta, the first of the Magnificent-7 to report, follow on Wednesday along

### Page 5

with Lam Research, IBM, and Texas Instruments. Spotify, Bloom Energy,
Western Digital, Lockheed Martin, and Blackstone report on Thursday.
ASML and TSMC posted record Q3 results last week. ASML’s net sales
rose to €11.7 billion from €9.3 billion in Q2, with the gross margin up to
57.5% from 54.0%. TSMC’s revenue jumped 50.9% y/y. Analysts’
consensus estimates imply Q3-2026 operating EPS growth for S&P 500
companies of 24.0% y/y (chart).
(4) Global. China’s Q3 GDP (Mon) follows Q2’s 4.3% y/y growth (chart).

We expect a moderate pickup in activity. China’s unemployment rate for
September is also due, following August’s 5.2% rate.
UK CPI (Wed) for September follows August’s 3.1% y/y headline and
2.6% core (chart). Rising energy and input costs point to a further pickup,

### Page 6

with the input PPI up 8.2% y/y in Q2. Bank of England Governor Andrew
Bailey speaks later that day. UK labor market data (Tue) and PPI (Wed)
are also due.
Canada’s CPI (Mon) for September follows August’s 3.0% y/y headline
and 2.3% core (chart).

EUROZONE: IMPROVING BUSINESS SENTIMENT, DRIVEN BY TECH, IS SPREADING TO OTHER SECTORS
Stéphane Colliac & Tarik Rharrab
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
The September PMI surveys confirm that growth in the Eurozone is getting stronger, boosted in particular by new export orders, which are
still rising. Sectors that were already performing well – technology and capital goods (including for defence) – are continuing to grow, while

those that had been struggling until now – aeronautics, consumer goods and financial services – are seeing their production indices recover.
This strong performance is particularly noteworthy, given that it has not yet been undermined by higher inflation.
The heatmap shows the PMI index readings: a reading above 50 indicates expansion, while a reading below 50 signals contraction compared to the previous
month. For each indicator, sectors are ranked in descending order based on their September 2026 reading.

Shades of red represent values above 50, while shades of blue represent values below 50: the intensity of the colour increases the further away they are
from this threshold.
Production growth spreads to more sectors
The PMI surveys on business sentiment confirm that business activity in the Eurozone is expanding at a faster pace and spreading to more sectors.
Household-consumption-related sectors (goods and services), which were adversely affected in the spring by the outbreak of the conflict in the Middle

East, do not appear to be negatively impacted by the rising inflation observed since August. The aeronautics sector, where production has mainly been
limited by supply constraints, is also showing signs of improvement.

These trends, although still moderate, come alongside a drive to upgrade equipment, fuelled by investment in digital technology, electrification
(which is supporting the automotive sector) and defence. The momentum in technology goods and services further strengthened in September.
The production index for computer and electronics hit a five-year high, while the software services production index stayed close to its July peak.

Financial services are also enjoying a significant improvement, reflecting the healthy lending momentum despite interest rates starting to rise
(see our analysis).
Growth driven by exports
Exports drove growth in the first half of 2026, both across the Eurozone as a whole (see our analysis) and among its key members (notably
Germany). This momentum is also increasing, as indicated by the ‘new export orders’ component of the various PMI indices. As was the case with

production, there has been a particularly noteworthy increase for capital goods (technology and machinery) and related services, particularly digital
services. This trend is playing out more broadly, as, in September, new export orders rose across all sub-sectors, with the exception of intermediate
goods and consumer goods, where contraction recently eased. There has been a significant improvement in the transport sector, particularly in
the aeronautics industry, which has major markets in Asia. Recent data on French exports show that aeronautics exports to this region are rising

once again.
The convergence between rising production and increasing export-order backlogs shows that Eurozone growth is based on an increasingly solid
foundation. Inflationary pressures are not holding it back for the time being.

Document 378: SpaceX Q3 AI业务大爆发、收入暴增830%，利润碾压预期，资本支出近200亿｜财报见闻

Source PDF: all report pdf/SpaceX Q3 AI业务大爆发、收入暴增830%，利润碾压预期，资本支出近200亿｜财报见闻.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

三季度， SpaceX 总营收同⽐增 161% ⾄近 146 亿美元，净利润 44 亿，分别较分析师预期⾼
14% 和 134% ，资本⽀出较营收⾼逾三成、基本符合预期；云服务、收购 Cursor 和 Grok 应
⽤增⻓推动 AI 业务超级智能（ SI ）收⼊增⾄ 75.6 亿美元，⾸次成为公司最⼤收⼊源，较预
期⾼ 20% ，业务资本⽀出 164 亿、占总⽀出 84% ；互联业务收⼊同⽐增 69% ，星链⽤⼾增
⾄ 1360 万；航天业务收⼊增 6% ，星舰⾸次贡献收⼊，但研发投⼊继续拖累，业务运营亏损
700 万美元。财报后，股价盘后转涨，⼀度涨近 7% 。
摘要：
上市以来第⼆份季度财报显⽰，⻢斯克旗下 SpaceX 今年第三季度取得了碾压华尔街预期的亮眼
成绩。
美东时间 20 ⽇周⼆美股盘后， SpaceX 公布，截⾄ 9 ⽉的 2026 年第三季度，公司营收同⽐增⻓
161% ⾄ 145.8 亿美元，较分析师预期的约 128 亿美元⾼ 10% 以上；净利润约 44 亿美元，为分析师

预期 18.8 亿美元的 2.3 倍；调整后 EBITDA 利润同⽐⼤增 306% ⾄ 88.1 亿美元，较预期⾼逾 30% 。
这份财报最突出的变化是， SpaceX 的增⻓引擎已经从传统⽕箭发射和 “ 星链 ” （ Starlink ）互联业
务，进⼀步转向 AI 领域业务 “ 超级智能 ” （下称 SI ）。三季度 SI 收⼊达到 75.6 亿美元，同⽐暴增
830% ，较分析师预期的 63 亿美元⾼ 20% ，并⾸次成为公司最⼤收⼊来源。
相⽐⼆季度， SpaceX 增⻓斜率明显抬升。⼆季度公司营收同⽐增⻓ 92% ，净亏损 5.41 亿美元，
虽较⼀年前 10 亿美元的亏损明显收窄，但仍未实现盈利；当季调整后 EBITDA 为 35 亿美元，同⽐
增⻓ 191% 。到了三季度，公司不仅营收同⽐增速跃升⾄ 161% ，还实现 40 亿多美元净利润，调
整后 EBITDA 环⽐也增⻓约 149% 。
财报公布前，美东时间 10 ⽉ 20 ⽇周⼆， SpaceX （ SPCX ）股价收跌约 0.1% 。盘后 SpaceX 公布

财报后，股价迅速转涨，盘后⼀度涨超 2% 。市场反应的核⼼在于： SI 收⼊和利润释放速度超预
期，抵消了资本开⽀和成本继续⾼企带来的压⼒。
业绩电话会期间，随着⻢斯克等公司⾼管透露明年年度经常性收⼊（ ARR ）最⾼ 1 万亿美元、实
现 13GW AI 算⼒等⽬标或指引，还确认在讨论与特斯拉合并， SpaceX 股价进⼀步⾛⾼，盘后涨
幅曾接近 7% 。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

核⼼指标：营收、利润、 EBITDA 全⾯超预期，但成本也⾼于预期
从彭博提供的分析师预期看， SpaceX 三季度⼏乎在收⼊和利润端全⾯跑赢市场预期。
指标 三季度实际 分析师预期 对⽐
营收 145.8 亿美元 约 128 亿美元 超预期约 14%
SI/AI 收⼊ 75.6 亿美元 63 亿美元 超预期约 20%
运营利润 27.9 亿美元 22.4 亿美元 超预期约 25%
每股收益（ EPS ） 0.35 美元 0.15 美元 超预期逾⼀倍
净利润 44.0 亿美元 18.8 亿美元 超预期约 134%
调整后 EBITDA 88.1 亿美元 65.9 亿美元 超预期约 34%
资本开⽀ 195.9 亿美元 196 亿美元 基本符合预期
SI 资本开⽀ 164.4 亿美元 169 亿美元 略低于预期
总成本与开⽀ 117.8 亿美元 112.3 亿美元 ⾼于预期约 5%
公司三季度总营收从去年同期的 55.8 亿美元增⾄ 145.8 亿美元；总成本与开⽀则从 58.5 亿美元增

⾄ 117.8 亿美元。虽然成本⾼于预期，但收⼊扩张更快，带动运营利润从去年同期亏损 2.73 亿美
元转为盈利 27.9 亿美元，运营利润率达到约 19% 。
调整后 EBITDA 为 88.1 亿美元，对应调整后 EBITDA 利润率约 60% 。需要注意的是，调整后
EBITDA 为⾮ GAAP 指标，剔除了折旧摊销、股权激励、并购相关费⽤等项⽬，因此其改善幅度
明显⾼于 GAAP ⼝径下运营利润。
SI 成最⼤收⼊来源：云服务、 Cursor 和 Grok 推动 AI 业务爆发
SpaceX 财报中最关键的变量是 SI 业务。公司注释称， SI 即此前披露的⼈⼯智能（ Artificial
Intelligence ）业务。
三季度 SI 收⼊ 75.6 亿美元，环⽐增⻓ 195% ，同⽐增⻓ 830% ，已经超过互联（ Connectivity ）业
务的 52.5 亿美元，成为 SpaceX 第⼀⼤业务板块。其中：

### Page 3

⼴告收⼊ 5.38 亿美元，同⽐增⻓约 19% ；
SI 解决⽅案与基础设施收⼊ 70.22 亿美元，去年同期仅 3.59 亿美元；
公司称， SI 解决⽅案和基础设施收⼊同⽐增加 67 亿美元，主要来⾃新的云服务协议、 Cursor
收购，以及 Grok 模型和软件应⽤增⻓。
盈利能⼒也出现拐点。 SI 三季度运营利润 4.31 亿美元，⽽⼆季度仍亏损 12.57 亿美元，去年同期
亏损 17.22 亿美元。公司披露，三季度 SI 运营利润中包含 Cursor 收购带来的 12 亿美元股权激励费
⽤。若按公司⼝径， SI 调整后 EBITDA 达到 49.53 亿美元，去年同期为亏损 4.28 亿美元。
算⼒扩张仍是 SI 业务的核⼼。 SpaceX 三季度末名义算⼒达到 1.7GW ，⾼于⼆季度末的 1.4GW ，
也远⾼于去年同期的 0.6GW 。公司称，增⻓来⾃ COLOSSUS II 继续建设，并在季度内签署更多
云服务协议，增加⾯向第三⽅客⼾的 SI 基础设施容量储备。
Cursor 也是本季度财报的重点。 SpaceX 于 8 ⽉ 14 ⽇完成对 Cursor 的收购，隐含股权价值 600 亿

美元， GAAP 购买对价 425 亿美元，并发⾏与未来服务相关的 234 亿美元股权奖励。⾃并表⽇⾄ 9
⽉ 30 ⽇， Cursor 为 SpaceX 贡献 6.08 亿美元收⼊。
互联：星链⽤⼾增⾄ 1360 万，企业和政府收⼊增⻓更快
互联（ Connectivity ）仍是 SpaceX 最稳定的利润来源。三季度互联收⼊ 52.48 亿美元，环⽐增⻓
22% ，同⽐增⻓ 69% ；运营利润 23.67 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 99% ；调整后 EBITDA 为 34.99 亿美元，
同⽐增⻓ 82% 。
分项看，星链消费端仍在扩张：

### Page 4

消费者收⼊ 31.21 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 57% ；
企业与政府收⼊ 21.27 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 91% ；
星链消费订阅⽤⼾达到 1360 万，环⽐增加 160 万，去年同期为 720 万；
消费端⽉度 ARPU 为 69 美元，较⼆季度的 66 美元环⽐回升约 5% ，但低于去年同期的 83 美元。
这意味着，星链⽤⼾规模继续快速增⻓，但单⽤⼾收⼊仍低于去年⽔平。公司正在通过企业、
政府、航空、海事等⾼价值场景弥补消费端 ARPU 下滑。截⾄三季度末，已有超过 3800 架⻜机
搭载星链服务。
互联业务的成本也在上升。该板块总成本和开⽀同⽐增加 9.74 亿美元，主要来⾃卫星星座扩张、
星链终端交付以及⽀持收⼊增⻓的营销投⼊。但由于收⼊增⻓更快，该板块运营利润率达到约
45% ，仍是 SpaceX 当前最成熟的现⾦利润来源。
航天：收⼊增⻓相⽐其他业务温和，星舰⾸次贡献收⼊但研发投⼊继续拖
累利润
传统的航天（ Space ）业务三季度收⼊ 17.67 亿美元，环⽐增⻓ 84% ，同⽐增⻓ 6% ，⼤致与分析

师预期的约 18 亿美元业务收⼊⼀致。
不过，从运营数据看，发射数量并没有增⻓。三季度 SpaceX 完成：
客⼾发射 12 次，去年同期 15 次；
内部发射 23 次，去年同期 29 次；
总发射 35 次，去年同期 44 次；
⼊轨质量 476 吨，去年同期 525 吨。
也就是说，航天业务收⼊增⻓并⾮来⾃发射次数增加，⽽更多来⾃发射服务结构、开发收⼊以
及 “ 星舰 ” （ Starship ）相关进展。
三季度最重要的业务⾥程碑是星舰⾸次收⼊产⽣任务。当季， SpaceX 完成星舰第 14 次⻜⾏，也
是⾸次轨道级星舰⻜⾏，将 26 颗星链 V3 卫星送⼊宽带星座。公司称，该任务增加的下⾏容量约
为⼀次 Falcon 9 星链发射的 10 倍。

### Page 5

但星舰研发仍在侵蚀航天业务利润。三季度航天业务运营亏损 700 万美元，⼆季度亏损 5.42 亿美
元，去年同期则盈利 2.59 亿美元。公司称，航天业务总成本和开⽀同⽐增加 3.67 亿美元，主要因
为继续加速星舰项⽬研发投⼊。
利润质量：净利润⾼于运营利润，⾮经营项⽬贡献不⼩
SpaceX 三季度运营利润为 27.91 亿美元，但净利润达到 44.04 亿美元，⾼于运营利润本⾝。这说
明净利润不仅来⾃主业改善，也受到⾮经营项⽬和税项的明显⽀撑。
三季度公司录得：
利息收⼊ 8.73 亿美元；
利息费⽤ 6.75 亿美元；
其他收⼊净额 10.25 亿美元；
所得税收益 3.90 亿美元。
这些项⽬合计对净利润形成正向贡献。因此，虽然 GAAP ⼝径下净利润⼤幅超预期，但投资者仍
需区分 “ 主业经营改善 ” 和 “ 财务及税项因素 ” 对净利润的贡献。
此外，股权激励费⽤仍然较⾼。三季度公司普通股权激励费⽤ 11.46 亿美元，并购相关股权激励
费⽤ 12.17 亿美元，合计超过 23 亿美元。这些费⽤在调整后 EBITDA 中被剔除，但会影响 GAAP

利润和股东摊薄。
三季度稀释后加权平均股本为 124.75 亿股，去年同期为 29.41 亿股，反映 IPO 、股权融资、并购
对价及员⼯激励带来的显著摊薄。在此背景下，公司稀释后 EPS 仍达到 0.35 美元，⾼于预期的
0.15 美元。
资本⽀出较营收⾼逾三成， SI 算⼒建设仍是现⾦消耗核⼼
SpaceX 三季度资本开⽀ 195.93 亿美元，⼏乎与分析师预期的 196 亿美元⼀致，但绝对值极⾼，
较当季营收⾼约 34% 。其中 SI 业务资本开⽀占总资本开⽀约 84% ，略低于分析师预期的 169 亿美
元。
分业务板块看：
航天业务资本开⽀ 16.06 亿美元；
互联业务资本开⽀ 15.43 亿美元；
SI 资本开⽀ 164.44 亿美元。
这显⽰ SpaceX 当前资本开⽀⼏乎完全围绕 AI/SI 算⼒基础设施展开。 SI 单季收⼊ 75.6 亿美元，但
资本开⽀达到 164.4 亿美元，说明公司仍处在明显的提前建设产能阶段。
从前三季度看， SpaceX 经营活动现⾦流为 130.97 亿美元，但同期资本开⽀达到 480.69 亿美元，

简单计算⾃由现⾦流约为负 349.7 亿美元。公司增⻓仍需要资本市场和债务融资⽀持。
资产负债表⽅⾯， SpaceX 三季度末现⾦及现⾦等价物 512.94 亿美元，另有可交易证券 385.61 亿
美元，合计接近 899 亿美元。债务和融资租赁合计约 388 亿美元，公司仍处于净现⾦状态。前三

### Page 2

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?
Institutional adoption of stablecoins continues to incrementally progress in 2026. While
the frenzy from GENIUS has diminished, stablecoins’ links to the financial system have
continued to deepen, and more policymakers are seriously considering their use cases.
The market cap has hovered around ~$300bn over the past 12 months, a sign that the

stablecoin market is maturing. Stablecoin payments, while still marginal relative to
stablecoins' crypto-native purpose, are also growing, rising from around $103bn in
adjusted transaction volume in 2022 to $375bn in 2026 YTD.
For now, institutional adoption remains concentrated in cross-border payments, treasury
management, 24/7 liquidity provision, collateral, and real-time settlement.
The next big question is whether stablecoins will evolve to become merely a payments

technology, or a genuine disruptor to the global banking system. As we previously
established, the US promotion of stablecoins remains a geopolitical tool to ensure USD
dominance. Bloomberg also reported that the Trump administration is considering joint
ventures with private actors to promote USD stablecoins overseas, in a bid to boost the
dollar’s dominance and source demand for US Treasuries. Last week, Circle announced
plans to expand into 40 new markets in Southeast Asia, LatAm and the Middle East. 99%

of stablecoins (by market cap) remain pegged to USD and are largely backed by US
Treasuries.
Looking ahead, the key debate to watch is whether US regulations will allow for yield-
bearing stablecoins. Research shows that consumers say policymakers should prohibit
crypto companies from offering interest-like rewards for holding stablecoins, which will
threaten to siphon off the bank deposits that drive local lending. The US CLARITY Act
failed in Sep after lawmakers failed to agree on whether the broader crypto market

structure would support such yield-bearing stablecoins.
In this chartbook, we revisit the stablecoin market a year after the GENIUS Act was
signed. We look at: (i) how adoption has evolved, (ii) the emergence of stablecoins as
financial market plumbing via liquidity provisioning and collateral management, and (iii)
the key regulatory debates that will determine the industry’s next direction.
Payment volume has a +66% CAGR
Timeline of select events

### Page 3

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
REFRESHER: What are stablecoins?
– Definition: Digital tokens pegged to “stable” assets (e.g. USD or gold), issued
and transferred on a blockchain, which records ownership and transactions.
– Fiat-backed stablecoins are considered fully “collateralized”: USD
stablecoin with 1mn coins in circulation would be backed by $1mn in
cash and equivalents held in reserve by the stablecoin issuer.

– Types: Fiat-, commodity-, crypto- and algorithmic-backed.
– ~99% of stablecoins market valuation is pegged to the USD.
– Market cap: $5bn (2020) to ~$310bn (2026)
– Concentrated in USDT (~60%) and USDC (~23%).
– Benefits: 24/7 transfers & settlement, lower financial intermediaries,
programmability (e.g. escrow, conditional release, automated reconciliation),
and dollar-based store of value.
– Use cases:*
– Crypto trading (~77%) remains dominant.

– Payments (5-12%) growing, cross-border transfers and remittances.
– Store of value (17-33%) especially in emerging markets.
– Other: (~2%-8%): on/off ramps and tokenized RWA settlement.
– Alternatives: CBDCs, tokenized deposits, traditional financial architecture
*Estimates on market share taken from BCG analysis.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Haver Analytics, Bloomberg Finance LP. Shows stablecoin total
market cap. Updated October 192026.
Stablecoins’ reached a plateau of ~$310bn in October 2026

### Page 4

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
ADOPTION OVERVIEW: Most stablecoin volume is still not driven by real economic
activity
Stablecoin transaction volume:
– ~$100trn (unadjusted), ~$2.42trn (adjusted) in 2026 YTD, according to
Allium.
– Chainalysis places more bullish estimates of $28trn in 2025; projected to
reach $719trn by 2035 on organic growth alone.
Stablecoin payments:
– Stablecoin payment volume grew from $151bn in 2022 to $375bn 2026 YTD

(according to VisaOnChain Analytics).
– Businesses account for the majority (58%-64%) of payments, led by B2B
payment volumes.
– Allium Sep report showed largest business payment categories include
service fees ($56bn), payroll ($43bn) and service payments ($32bn).
– Case study: Payments volume across Visa’s >160 stablecoin-linked card
programs across consumer, business and commercial activity grew ~200%
y/y.
Strongest adoption in remittances, B2B payments and dollar access in countries

with unstable currencies or weak banking systems.
– Benefits for cross-border use cases: Instant settlement, <$0.10 per
transaction, borderless, transparent, 24/7, no intermediaries. Remittance fees
are lower (0.5-3.0%) than the global fee average (~6.6%).
– Stablecoins represented 0.31% of fiat cross-border payment value in 2025.
Marginal portion but registered +64% growth increase vs fiat (+9%) according
to Allium. B2B payments had the highest cross-border share (43%).

– A 2025 IMF study also shows transaction flows reached  ~7.7% of GDP in
Latin America and ~6.7% in Africa and the Middle East.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Allium. Shows top 8 countries by received payments from Jan to August 2026.
Only the receiving end is attributed, so a $100 payment counts once, not twice. That attribution covers 13.5% of
stablecoin payments.
Stablecoins payment use case is steadily growing

### Page 5

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
CONSUMER/RETAIL PAYMENT: Still on the periphery
Consumer payments: Not yet there
– Retail adoption is still the most distant use case, especially amongst developed-market consumer credit card spend. A recent ABA survey found that only
24% of consumers think that stablecoins could provide meaningful benefits.
– Stablecoins still look more like a debit instrument than a credit product. They do not naturally replicate the consumer valueproposition of credit cards (i.e.

rewards, revolving credit, dispute rights, chargebacks, fraud protection and convenience), so there is limited incentive for developed-market consumers to
use them.
– Limited scope for payments also evident in wallets (see chart below). Very little of the stablecoin supply is being held as balances in consumer wallets.
Still, the number of retail payments is incrementally growing, with cross-border, emerging market use cases strongest
– Retail transaction volume: $2.6bn (2020) to $71bn (2024); $66bn YTD in 2026.*

– Strongest use in: (i) economies with hyperinflation; (ii) poor banking access; or (iii) high remittance fees.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Allium data.  Shows circulating supply by holder type (Sep 2026). Categories calculated as a percentage of total circulating supply. Note that wallets account for money being held vs
payments (money moving). Business wallets in this case refer to those holding smaller balances with quick turnover (i.e. payment services, merchants, gaming), while institutional wallets hold larger balances and turn them

over quickly, i.e. financial-services firms, treasuries, and wallets. Note: *Retail sized transactions are defined as transactions of $250 or less, according to Visa OnChain Analytics.
The amount of stablecoin supply in consumer wallets stands at ~14%

### Page 6

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
CASH/LIQUDITY MANAGEMENT: Stablecoins are evolving as a major tokenized
contender
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, taken from DB’s Tokenised money and the future of FXand Stablecoins and
agentic commerce: new rails, old frictions.
Stablecoins can help treasury management and settlement
– Stablecoins have a strong use case for settling transactions for FX,

treasury operations and collateral management i.e. intra-day repos.
– This is because stablecoins’ 24/7 settlement can address
operational frictions in cross-border transactions.
Poses significant risk to cross-border B2B transactions for banks
– Compared to traditional banks, stablecoins offer substantial relief
from transaction costs. Our equity analysts estimate total customer
processing fee savings to increase from 0 to ~$70bn in 2035, if
consumers switch from traditional banks to stablecoins.

Stablecoins is not a replacement solution to FX settlement risk
– FX settlement requires two currencies to be exchanged
simultaneously. Currently, stablecoins only serve as an intermediary
between the two currency legs, resulting in a “stablecoin sandwich”.
– Overwhelming concentration of USD-stablecoins also limits
stablecoin ability to support widespread payment-versus payment
settlement across currency pairs.
– Stablecoins also face fragmented interoperability/liquidity issues

and regulatory uncertainty across issuers/networks/jurisdictions.
– For more, read our FX report here.
Should we explain how settlement work as it is very
interesting and Rohini has a nice chart on this ?
Probably more relevant than having the stablecoin
market cap here I would say.
A more integrated post-trade process

### Page 7

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
INSTITUTIONAL ADOPTION: Corporates, card networks, and banks are integrating
stablecoins
Institutions are racing to capture stablecoin market share
– Card networks: Mastercard expanded its settlement capabilities to include
stablecoins, using USDC and Paxos-issued stablecoins.
– Institutions: Exploring stablecoins via developing in-house permissioned

blockchain experiments and/or exploring issuance of proprietary stablecoins.
– >140 companies and financial institutions helped launch Open USD
(OUSD) in Sep 2026. OUSD reached $799mn of supply in 2 weeks.
Signals shift toward consortium-backed/network-led digital dollar
models.
Are stablecoins posing a threat to traditional payment systems?
– Bullish: Stablecoins to surpass traditional rails by the 2030s (Chainalysis).
– ~17% of stablecoin-linked card volume is occurring across Visa’s

business and commercial card programs. Shows growing adoption of
stablecoins for settlement, treasury management and cross-border
commerce.
– Key concerns for banks: Fee pressure from stablecoin adoption →
bank outflows → higher reliance on wholesale funding → pressure on
net interest margins.
– Base case: However, Deutsche Bank equity analysts see more limited near-
term risk. Visa and Mastercard to remain dominant especially in advanced
markets.

– KYC, AML, sanctions screening, wallet monitoring, fraud controls and
jurisdiction-specific limits remain essential for banks.
Stablecoin transactions could match Visa and Mastercard transactions
sometime between 2031 and 2039 (Chainalysis).
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Chainalysis.
Shows projected years until crypto merchant transactions reach Visa scale.

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
GEOPOLITICS: Why stablecoins are a "Genius" idea for the US
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, US Treasury, Tether July 2026 report. Shows Major foreign holders of Treasury Securities.
Treasury data from June 2026.
Can sustain USD dominance
– 99% of stablecoins (by market cap) are USD-pegged.
– Stablecoins function primarily as a digital version of dollars

moving over blockchain rails, extending the dollar’s reach into
new digital environments.
– Reinforces dollar savings: The more firms invoice and transact in USD,
the more they save in USD assets. Stablecoins help preserve this
feedback loop by keeping businesses and individuals in dollar
payment rails.
Can support US debt, given share held by foreigners has fallen from
>50% in early 2000s to ~30% in early 2026.
– Stablecoin issuers total holdings: ~$170bn according to Allium x

Citizen.
– Tether: ~$141bn in direct and indirect exposure to US T-bills.
– Impact: BIS shows how $3.5bn inflow into dollar-backed stablecoins
lowers 3m T-bill yields by 2.5-3.5bp under normal conditions. Effect
more than doubles in periods of T-bill shortages.
– Forecasts: T-bills holding by stablecoin issuers could reach ~$1trn by
2028 (Treasury forecasts).
– Recent Fed SF research places it closer to $400bn by the end
of 2030, lower than the US government’s financing needs.

Stablecoin issuers’  demand for US debt could counterweight declining demand from China
Tether competes with Saudi Arabia and South Korea as major holders of US Treasuries
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco, US Treasury Data. Shows Treasury
holdings, stablecoin issuers vs China.

### Page 10

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
US GENIUS ACT: Debate on rules around payment stablecoins one year on
GENIUS provisions
– Strict issuer requirements: Issuers can be private, non-bank entity, but must
meet strict federal or state requirements.
– Reserves: Requires 1:1 backing with US cash, 93 days T-bills, repos/reverse
repos, or money market funds holding those assets.
– AML/KYC/BSA compliance obligations to stablecoin issuers, aligning them

exams, and strong internal controls.
Next steps
– GENIUS takes effect no later than 18 Jan 2027, or 120 days after final
implementing regulations are issued.
– Ongoing pushback from US banks as to whether GENIUS ban on yield-
bearing stablecoins should extend to affiliate or third-party arrangements,
given many users do not hold stablecoins directly with the issuer.
– CLARITY ACT failed in Sep because of disagreements on how
exchanges/broader crypto market structure should handle stablecoins.

– The US Treasury has proposed implementing rules for payment stablecoins.
There are two ways stablecoin yield can be paid; ongoing debate over the three-
party model
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Congressional Research Service.

RISKS: Still hotly discussed amongst policymakers and financial institutions
Bank liquidity risk
Loss of stable funding source for banks from deposit outflow (not offset by
partial flowback from stablecoin issuers’ reserve deposit)
System stability risk
Stablecoins can fail to maintain their $1 value during times of stress. Evidence shows
that USDC and Tether actually trade within 1% of their dollar value but prices can

diverge sharply during stress episodes.
Run on stablecoin issuer(s) in market stress / trust scenario triggering fire sales of
reserve assets and/or deposit withdrawals.

❑ For USD stablecoins like
USDT/USDC mainly short-
term Treasury securities

stablecoins, e.g. gold with only
small market share

REGULATORY COMPARISON
Overview of stablecoin regulation in the US, UK and EU UKEU US
Regime Maturity
Key Timelines
Regulatory
Framework
US
The U.S. Genius Act
EU
Markets in Crypto-Asset Regulation
(MiCAR)
Backing Assets*
• 100% backing assets in USD cash or short-term
Treasuries (≤93-day T-bills).
• Non-significant EMTs –At least 30% reserves in
same-currency bank deposits, remainder in HQLA

• Significant EMTs - At least 60% of the reserves in
bank deposits; remainder in HQLA
• Systemic stablecoins – 30% unremunerated BoE
deposits vs 70% in UK sovereign debt (HQLA)
• Non-systemic – Full 1:1 backing in HQLR assets,
minimum 5% in on demand deposits.
UK
Multi-issuance
Stance
• Rules are still being fine-tuned; no specific focus
yet on multi-issuance.
• Multi-issuance under active review; EC considers multi-
issuance permissible, an equivalence-based framework for

EU/third-country arrangements is under legislative
consideration.
• BoE considers multi-issuance unsuitable for systemic
UK use. Preference for single-issuance models backed
by single reserve (incl. Non-GPB denom. stablecoins) .
UK Cryptoasset Regime (FCA)
UK Systemic Stablecoins Regime (BoE)
Eligible Issuers
• Bank subsidiaries, federal nonbank licensees, or
state issuers with federal registration, with
enhanced oversight for >$10bn circulation.

• EMTs: EU-authorised credit institutions or authorised
electronic money institutions. Restrictions apply to
non-EU currency EMTs (exceeding MiCAR
thresholds) as a means of exchange
• FCA-authorised firms for non-systemic stablecoins,
• Systemic issuers to be designated by HM Treasury
and jointly regulated under BoE/FCA oversight
Balance Sheet
Treatment
• Stablecoins treated as reserve-backed digital
payment assets, segregated in bankruptcy-remote

accounts and treated as cash equivalents
• EMTs are fiat-referenced cryptoassets subject to
MiCAR and e-money requirements
• (OCC/FDIC) Mandates prudential capital buffers
tailored to issuer risk profile.
• (Fed) Framework consistent with bank requirements
• EMI issuers: minimum own funds of 2% of EMT
supply; 3% for significant EMTs. Significant issuers
subject to enhanced risk-management requirements
• Systemic: 6 months operating expenses/largest

plausible loss event
• Non-systemic: 1% av. stablecoins in circulation
Capital Reserve
Requirements**

AUTHORS
Marion Laboure is a Managing Director and Macro Strategist at Deutsche Bank in London, and a Lecturer in Economics and Finance at
Harvard University.
She was previously an economist at the IMF, the Central Bank of Luxembourg, Barclays, and the European Commission. She has twice
received the First Prize of the American Society of Actuaries, was named a rising star in finance by Revue Banque, and is recognised

among the 45 standout women in fintech globally.
She holds a Bachelor's degree in Mathematics from the University of Paris Dauphine, a Master of Research in Economics from the
London School of Economics, and a PhD in Econometrics and Quantitative Economics from the École Normale Supérieure.
She is the author of Democratizing Finance published by Harvard University Press.
Camilla Siazon is an Associate on Deutsche Bank’s Macro and Thematic team in London.

She focuses on digital assets, geopolitics, and global macroeconomic trends. Prior to Deutsche Bank, she worked at geopolitical
advisory firms Eurasia Group and Greenmantle.
She holds a dual MA/MSc in International History from Columbia University and the London School of Economics, and a BA in History
from Barnard College, Columbia University.

### Page 3

The magnitude of S&P 500 “over-earning”

S&P 500 earnings grew by an extraordinary 51% year/year in Q2 2026 and have 
grown by 26% during the past four quarters. During the last 30 years, the recent pace 
of earnings growth has been exceeded only during the post-recession rebounds in 2010 
and 2021.

The recent strength of S&P 500 earnings growth has raised investor concerns that 
current earnings power is unsustainable and that the market is in an “earnings 
bubble.” S&P 500 earnings have recently outpaced both their historical trend and their 
historical relationship with US GDP growth. S&P 500 4-quarter EPS growth has averaged 
7% during the past 30 years, and the relationship between S&P 500 earnings and US 
economic growth in recent decades would have pointed to a similar rate of earnings 
growth during the last few quarters.

Exhibit 1: S&P 500 trailing 4-quarter EPS has grown by 26% during the past year

### Page 4

The degree to which the US equity market is currently “expensive” depends in large 
part on the sustainability of recent earnings strength. The exceptional recent 
strength of earnings has driven an unusual wedge between equity valuation multiples 
based on near-term earnings and valuations on trend earnings. Because equity prices 
have failed to keep pace with surging earnings, near-term valuations show no hint of a 
bubble: The S&P 500 12-month P/E ratio has declined from 23x a year ago to 19x today 
and now matches its 10-year average. However, even an “average” multiple may be 
expensive if current earnings are unsustainable. The cyclically-adjusted P/E based on 
trailing 10-year earnings has climbed to one of its highest levels on record, falling shy of 
the highs of 1999-2000 but surpassing the level reached in 2021. Similarly, our 
colleagues have argued that the increase in market value of AI companies during the last 
few years requires an optimistic estimate of the future magnitude of AI-related profits 
and who will capture them.

### Page 5

The AI boom and S&P 500 earnings growth

Affirming investor concerns that US companies are “over-earning,” there are three 
factors that are boosting earnings today but will contribute to deceleration going 
forward: 1) AI capex spending, 2) semiconductor margin expansion, and 3) earnings 
from private investment gains.

AI capex 
The AI investment boom is driving nearly half of S&P 500 EPS growth this year, but 
its contribution will fade going forward. The mega-cap US hyperscalers are on track to 
spend $800 billion on capex this year, an increase of 94% vs. 2025. That spending is 
flowing through to the earnings of the AI infrastructure complex including 
semiconductor, tech hardware, industrials, and utilities companies, which are collectively 
accounting for roughly half of consensus S&P 500 earnings growth this year. The AI 
boom is also having a secondary impact on earnings outside of the infrastructure 
complex, for example by boosting capital markets activity and consumer wealth. Both 
consensus and GS analyst forecasts show hyperscaler capex growing at a slower rate in 
coming years, which should result in decelerating earnings growth for much of the AI 
infrastructure complex and a fading tailwind to S&P 500 earnings.

### Page 6

Hyperscaler depreciation expenses will continue to increase as capex growth 
decelerates, further dampening the boost of AI investment spending to S&P 500 
earnings growth. We estimate a drag from hyperscaler depreciation expenses on S&P 
500 earnings growth of 5pp in 2027, offsetting nearly half of the 11pp boost to earnings 
from capex spending. By 2028, the drag from depreciation should offset the S&P 500 
earnings uplift from continued capex spending.

### Page 7

If capex were fully expensed, consensus 2027 S&P 500 EPS would be 22% lower than 
under current accounting rules. The current gap between actual reported and 
hypothetical fully-expensed earnings is one of the widest in recent decades, exceeded 
only as earnings fell more quickly than capex during the recessions of 2001 and 2008.

Hyperscaler capex has consistently surprised relative to consensus estimates during 
the last few years, and the potential for additional surprises going forward creates a 
wide range of potential S&P 500 earnings outcomes. Our equity analysts expect 
hyperscaler capex will total $1.2 trillion in 2027 and $1.4 trillion in 2028, in which case 
the current tailwind from AI capex to S&P 500 EPS will fade by 2028. Exhibit 10 below 
outlines upside and downside scenarios around that base case. Next year, a $250 billion 
surprise in hyperscaler capex — in either direction — would shift S&P 500 EPS growth by 
about 6 pp in the same direction.  
We estimate that hyperscaler capex would need to decline by roughly 30%, to $570 
billion, to offset earnings growth for the rest of the S&P 500 in 2027. This decline 
would reduce earnings for the AI infrastructure complex by about 40%, leaving their 
profits about 25% above 2025 levels.

### Page 8

Semiconductor margin expansion 
A sharp increase in profit margins for many semiconductor companies has 
contributed to the above-trend recent pace of S&P 500 earnings growth. The 
combination of strong demand and limited supply has boosted semiconductor prices 
and gross margins, with the most pronounced margin expansion among memory stocks. 
S&P 500 memory firms currently enjoy gross margins of roughly 80%, more than double 
their historical average. Across the semiconductor industry, gross margins sit at their 
highest level in decades, and consensus expects margins to remain at similar levels 
through 2028.

Margin expansion has accounted for a large share of recent semiconductor earnings 
growth, but that boost should fade going forward. We estimate that about 1/4 of S&P 
500 Semiconductor earnings growth in 2026 is being driven by gross margin expansion. 
Our equity analysts believe the balance of memory supply and demand is likely to 
remain tight through 2027, with incremental supply additions unlikely to impact industry 
margins until 2028 at the earliest. However, they expect the rate of incremental margin 
expansion to slow meaningfully next year, reducing the contribution of semiconductors

S&P 500 earnings growth in the next few years will be highly sensitive to the 
trajectory of semiconductor margins. In a scenario where slowing AI infrastructure 
investment, increasing supply, and/or technological shift lowers semiconductor prices 
and profit margins, S&P 500 EPS growth would also disappoint. We estimate that each 
percentage point change in S&P 500 semiconductor gross margins next year would shift 
S&P 500 EPS growth by about 1 pp.

### Page 10

“Other income” from private investment gains 
Appreciating equity investment stakes are also temporarily inflating S&P 500 
earnings. Mega-cap tech earnings have recently been boosted by large GAAP “other 
income” related to their equity investments. In Q2 2026, the mega-cap tech companies 
enjoyed unrealized investment gains in private companies that totaled roughly $150 
billion, translating into 12% of S&P 500 EPS. We expect H2 2026 reports will include 
additional “other income” that should be larger than the gains in Q1 but smaller than Q2 
totals. We expect a much smaller contribution in 2027. The complete removal of this 
“other income” next year would create a drag of 8 pp on S&P 500 earnings growth in 
2027 relative to 2026, all else equal.

### Page 11

S&P 500 earnings outlook

Our baseline forecast is for S&P 500 EPS to rise to $415 in 2027 (+11% year/year) 
and to $460 in 2028 (+11%). We expect an above-consensus base of earnings in 2026 
due to further “other income” gains from private equity stakes in H2 of this year. 
Excluding the “other income” inflating earnings in 2026, S&P 500 earnings growth next 
year would register 18%. Our 2027 EPS forecast is similar to the bottom-up consensus 
estimate of $419 but above the median top-down strategist estimate of $403.

### Page 12

Our 2028 EPS forecast is about 5% below the bottom-up consensus, signaling a 
return to the typical historical pattern of downward revisions to consensus earnings 
estimates after the last two years of rising estimates. Outside of the complex of AI 
capex beneficiaries, we expect sales growth will register close to nominal GDP , margins 
will expand modestly, and a growing boost from AI productivity will result in earnings 
growth of 9% in both 2027 and 2028.  Our forecasts for EPS growth among this group of 
companies are roughly 4-5% below consensus estimates in both 2027 and 2028, similar 
to the historical average magnitude of downward revision to consensus estimates. While 
AI investment spending has had a direct impact on the earnings of semiconductor and 
other AI infrastructure stocks, it has also lifted earnings for companies outside of the “ AI 
complex,” including by boosting capital markets activity as well as supporting consumer 
spending via the wealth effect. 
Solid GDP growth and easing pressure from energy prices will be the main macro 
tailwinds for earnings growth in 2027. Economic growth is the primary variable in our 
top-down earnings model and explains more than half of the historical variation in S&P 
500 EPS growth. Our economists expect US real GDP growth will average 2.1% in 2027 
and 2.3% in 2028. Our commodity strategists expect Brent crude oil will decline to 
$80/bbl by the end of 2027, which would weigh modestly on Energy sector profits but 
boost earnings for most other sectors. Outside of the AI-related risks outlined above, 
persistent input cost pressures and risks to the consumer from equity market volatility 
are two of the key macro downside risks to our earnings forecast.

### Page 13

As the earnings tailwind from AI investment spending fades, the trajectory of S&P 
500 profitability in coming years will depend increasingly on the realization of AI 
productivity gains. Our forecasts embed a 1 pp boost to S&P 500 EPS growth from AI 
productivity in 2027 and a 2 pp boost in 2028. Economy-wide surveys and corporate 
commentary signal enterprise adoption that is increasing but remains in early stages, 
and the impact of AI adoption on corporate earnings still appears narrow. However, the 
recent acceleration in enterprise AI spending suggests that the earnings impact of AI 
adoption should become clearer in coming quarters.

### Page 14

Valuing S&P 500 earnings

Like our forecasts and consensus estimates, market pricing suggests investors 
anticipate a deceleration in earnings growth going forward. This is true both for the 
broad market and for AI infrastructure stocks. For example, the share prices of 
semiconductor stocks have historically mirrored the path of consensus EPS estimates, 
and the recent moderation of semiconductor returns has aligned with consensus 
estimates for decelerating growth in 2027 and 2028.

The current S&P 500 P/E multiple signals an optimistic outlook but market 
skepticism regarding the sustainability of current profits. In addition to rising interest 
rates, an anticipated deceleration in earnings helps explain the recent decline in the S&P 
500 forward P/E multiple. Along with macro factors like interest rates, the level of 
corporate profitability has helped explain the variation in S&P 500 valuation over time. 
Based on the historical relationship between S&P 500 ROE and P/E, the current S&P 500 
ROE of 24% — a record high — would point to a forward 12-month P/E of over 21x 
against today’s macro backdrop. Given current inflation and interest rates, today’s S&P 
500 multiple of 19x would be consistent with an ROE of roughly 22%, more than 200 bp 
below the current level but otherwise matching 2021 as the highest on record.

### Page 15

Earnings, rather than valuations, should continue to drive equity market upside 
from here. Our 3-, 6-, and 12-month S&P 500 return targets are +5 (8000), +9% (8300), 
and +14% (8700). These imply a P/E multiple on consensus forward EPS that remains 
close to the current level of 19x. Our economists and interest rate strategists expect 
interest rates to decline modestly in coming quarters, creating some macro upside risk to 
equity valuations. However, we expect investor uncertainty regarding the impact of AI 
on the outlook for long-term profits to persist in coming quarters, making a dramatic 
increase in valuations unlikely.

Document 437: 「社外取締役が議長」は何を変えるのか ～プライム市場で118社、大型企業で選任進む～

Source PDF: all report pdf/「社外取締役が議長」は何を変えるのか ～プライム市場で118社、大型企業で選任進む～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

Flash Insights

2026 年 10 月 8 日

1/4 
「社外取締役が議長」は何を変えるのか 
～プライム市場で 118 社、大型企業で選任進む～ 
 資産運用調査部 シニア研究員  河谷 善夫

1．社外取締役の取締役会議長選任は緩やかに拡大 
筆者は、昨年8 月中旬時点でのプライム市場上場会社のうち、社外取締役を取締役会議長に選
任している会社について分析したレポート（河谷、2025）を公表し、2022 年 7 月上旬時点より、
その数が増加していることを示した。本稿では、2026 年9 月末の時点でのプライム市場における
社外取締役の取締役会議長選任の状況をフォローアップした上で、最後に社外取締役の取締役会
議長選任の意味について考える。

資料 1 社外取締役の取締役会議長選任会社数の推移

（出所）東証公表資料を基に第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

資料 1 のとおり、2025 年 8 月から2026 年 9 月にかけて会社数は9 社増加し、プライム市場での
比率は 6.7％から7.6％と0.9％ポイント上昇した。2022 年以降、年平均で概ね1％ポイントの上
昇ペースが続いている。このグラフだけでは見えないが、2025 年8 月から2026 年9 月にかけて継
続してプライム市場に上場している会社では17 社が社外取締役を新たに取締役会議長に選任して
おり（注1）、新規選任の動きは続いている。 
また、118 名中 117 名が独立社外取締役であり、社外取締役議長はほぼすべて独立社外取締役で
ある（注2）。

### Page 2

2/4 
2．特定の機関設計に限らず普及、ただし社外取締役議長はなお少数 
資料 2 は、会社の機関設計別の2022 年、2025 年、2026 年のプライム市場での社外取締役の取
締役会議長選任会社数と割合を示したものである。

資料 2 機関設計別の社外取締役の取締役会議長選任状況（プライム市場）

（出所）資料 1 と同じ

2026 年では、社外取締役を取締役会議長に選任している会社は、監査役設置会社で5.3％、監
査等委員会設置会社で6.0％、指名委員会等設置会社で42.7％である。指名委員会等設置会社の
割合は依然として高いが、2022 年からの増加状況をみると、社外取締役を取締役会議長とする動
きは、指名委員会等設置会社以外にも徐々に広まっており、昨年のレポートで確認した傾向は続
いているといえる。 
なお、これも資料で示していないが、取締役の過半を独立社外取締役とする会社でも、社外取
締役を取締役会議長とする会社は約2 割にとどまり、2025 年 8 月から2026 年 9 月にかけて大きな
変化はなかった（注3）。

3．社長を議長とする会社の減少と、大型企業で先行する社外取締役議長選任 
資料 3 では、2022 年、2025 年、2026 年のプライム市場での取締役会議長の属性の変化をグラフ
として示した。

資料 3 取締役会議長の属性の変化（プライム市場）

（注）「その他の取締役等」には、会長・社長以外の執行役を兼任する取締役を含む。 
（出所）資料 1 と同じ

### Page 3

3/4 
2022 年からの変化をみると、社長を取締役会議長とする会社の割合は71.4％から65.6％へ
5.8％ポイント低下する一方、社外取締役を議長とする会社の割合は、3.6％から 7.6％へ4.0％ポ
イント上昇し、会長を議長とする会社の割合も1.5％ポイント上昇している。このような社長を取
締役会議長とする会社の割合の低下、社外取締役を議長とする会社の割合の上昇は、取締役会議
長と社長を分離するという、より大きな流れの一部とみることができるだろう。 
次に、どのような会社で社外取締役の取締役会議長選任が進んでいるのかをみるため、2026 年
9 月末時点のプライム市場上場会社について、時価総額ランク別の選任割合を示した（資料4）。

資料 4 時価総額ランク別社外取締役議長選任割合（プライム市場・2026 年 9 月末）

（出所）Bloomberg、東証公表資料データを基に第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

資料 4 からは、時価総額の大きい企業ほど、社外取締役の取締役会議長選任が進んでいるとい
える。なお、社外取締役を取締役会議長に選任している会社の社数の割合は7.6％だが、時価総額
では 19％を占める。また、社外取締役が議長となっている会社の39％が時価総額１兆円以上の会
社である。時価総額が大きい企業ほど選任率が高く、大型企業での社外取締役の取締役会議長選
任が相対的に進んでいる。

4．最後に 
ここまで、社外取締役の取締役会議長選任状況について確認してきた。このような社外取締役
の取締役会議長選任はコーポレートガバナンス上、どのような意味を持つのか、最後に私見を述
べたい。 
とりわけ独立社外取締役が議長を務める場合には、独立した立場から取締役会の議論を促し、
監督機能を含む取締役会の実効性向上につながることが期待される。そのためには、経営陣との
適切な連絡・調整や十分な情報提供が重要となる。今回のコーポレートガバナンス・コード改訂
でも、議長や独立社外取締役を支える取締役会事務局（コーポレートセクレタリー等）の機能強

### Page 4

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
4/4 
化の重要性が示されている。社外取締役を議長に選任する企業には、選任によってどのような効
果を目指すのかを明確にし、それに即した支援体制を整えることが重要であろう。 
以 上

【注釈】 
1) 2025 年8 月中旬のプライム市場上場会社（1,618 社）のうち、社外取締役を取締役会議長に選
任している会社数の異同については次のとおり。2026 年 9 月末までの間にプライム市場から退
出した会社（95 社）で社外取締役を取締役会議長に選任していた7 社と、継続上場会社（1,523
社）で社外取締役以外を取締役会議長にした会社1 社の計8 社が減少。継続上場会社のうち17 社
が新規に社外取締役を取締役会議長に選任したので、差し引き9 社の増加となった。新規にプライ
ム市場に上場した会社（20 社）には、社外取締役を取締役会議長に選任していた会社はなかった。 
2) なお、2026 年 9 月末でプライム市場上場会社の全社外取締役数は、6,986 名であり、そのうち
6,793 名（97.2％）が独立社外取締役である。ほとんどの社外取締役は独立社外取締役である
といってよい状況となっている。 
3) 一方、取締役の過半数を独立社外取締役とする会社が着実に増加していることは、河谷
（2026）で示したとおりである。

《歐元區經濟較預期具韌性，通膨壓力上升》
歐元區經濟在面臨中東衝突引發的能源成本飆升
下仍具韌性 。最新公布的9月歐元區綜合 PMI初值

升至53.1，超出預期 ，其中 ，製造業 PMI持平在
52.7，服務業PMI回升至53.0。個別國家中，德國
綜合PMI初值回升至 53.8，以2025年10月以來最
快速度擴張 ，AI與國防支出增加提供推動力 。然
而，能源價格居高不下 、天然氣價格漲幅更甚原
油，加大歐元區通膨壓力 ，投入成本和產出價格
均以四個月來最大幅度上漲。
從美伊雙方的態度來看 ，中東地緣僵局以及由此
帶來的能源價格壓力可能會持續一段時間 。歐元
區7月工業生產與零售銷售數據均較為疲軟 ，若能
源價格持續維持高檔 ，恐將推升企業成本並壓縮
家庭實質所得，對製造業與消費需求帶來壓力。
整體而言 ，歐元區經濟仍具韌性 ，但能源價格上
升所帶來的通膨與需求壓力 ，將是後續景氣的重
要觀察變數。

一碼 ，12月再次升息可能性
提高。
《歐洲央行9月會議如預期升息一碼，12月再次升
息可能性提高》
歐洲央行為了抑制通膨 ，2026年以來已升息二碼 ，
將存款機制利率上調至 2.5%。在歐央最新基準情
境預測中，2026、2027年歐元區GDP成長率預測
上修至0.9%、1.4%，然而，中東衝突持續、荷姆
茲海峽航運受阻及能源價格持續波動下 ，歐元區
經濟前景仍高度不確定。
通膨預估將在更長時間高於政策目標 ，基準情境
預估通膨率將在 4Q26升至3.6%高峰後，2Q27降
至2.5%。能源價格上升的影響將逐步傳導至非能
源價格 ，核心通膨預估 1Q27升至2.8%峰值後才
緩步回落，2027、2028年核心通膨率預測分別上

修0.1個百分點至 2.6%、2.3%，高於目標水準 。
不過，透過薪資傳導的二輪效應預期仍有限。
後續歐央政策將取決於能源價格衝擊的強度與持
續時間 ，以及是否進一步傳導至核心通膨 、薪資
與通膨預期。本行預期9月升息後，10月會議歐洲
央行將傾向觀望 ，然而，布蘭特原油期貨價格維
持在90美元/桶以上高檔 ，歐洲TTF天然氣期貨價
格漲幅更甚原油 ，在經濟維持韌性下 ，能源價格
維持高檔時間拉長 、核心通膨顯現能源價格的間
接傳導若增強 ，本行預估12月會議歐洲央行高機
率將再升息一碼，將存款機制利率升至2.75%。
資料來源：ECB，2026/9/10，中國信託商業銀行彙整，2026/9。

• 公債殖利率跳升對歐股評價

《公債殖利率跳升，歐股承壓》
九月份以來全球公債殖利率大幅攀升，對歐股造成
壓力。德國十年期公債殖利率9月份至9/28止跳升
32個基點至3.64%，創2009年6月以來新高。若從
2月底中東地緣衝突爆發以來起算 ，德國十年債利
率累積彈升了近100個基點。本波歐洲公債殖利率
走高來自多重因素疊加，能源價格上漲推升通膨、
市場重估歐央貨幣政策路徑，同時，財政支出令歐
洲公債供給增加與歐央持續縮表使私人部門需要吸
收更多公債供給，推升期限溢價，財政疑慮使部分
歐洲國家利差承受額外壓力，加上全球利率上升的
外溢影響，共同推升殖利率走高。
公債殖利率彈升令歐洲股市評價受到壓抑 ，9月份

以來至9/28止，STOXX歐洲600指數下跌近 2%。

展望10月份，美伊衝突的進展以及能源價格和債券
殖利率的走勢、企業財報、 預算協商將是盤面焦點。

差已跳升至 112.7個基點，創2012年6月以來新高 ，
主要反映市場對法國財政狀況的擔憂，今年第二季
法國政府債務佔GDP比重已逼近120%。

法國總理勒克努將於 10/1提出2027年預算案 ，法
國政府目標力拚節省540億歐元開支、將2027年財
政赤字佔GDP的比重降至5%。在政治局勢高度分
裂、2027年將舉行法國大選之際，此計劃將面臨強
大的政治阻力。無論是2027年預算討論、法國大選
民調的變化，相關不確定性預估將牽動短期內十年
期法德利差維持在約100個基點的高檔，無明顯回
落空間。

周期，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9。

供市場流動性 ，儘管不至於對廣泛金融體系構成
系統性風險 ，但短線土耳其股市 ，尤其是小型股
的波動預估仍將較為劇烈。

证券研究报告 | 宏观深度

宏观研究 
 【粤开宏观】回升中的分化：三季度
经济回顾与四季度展望 
2026 年 10 月 19 日  投资要点

近期报告  
《 【粤开宏观】前三季度经济形势快评》
2026-10-19 
《 【粤开宏观】9 月出口再提速： 涨价支撑
AI 领 跑 ， 汽 车 与 消 费 品 增 速 回 升 》
2026-10-14 
《 【粤开宏观】 中欧贸易新格局： 竞争、 摩
擦与合作》2026-10-11 
《 【粤开宏观】 文旅热潮之下， 消费潜力为
何未充分释放？ ——中国文旅消费的特
征、问题与制度成因》2026-10-07 
《 【粤开宏观】300 亿美元对等降税安排：
美国对华关税的“变”与“不变”》2026-09-27

摘要 
对当前宏观经济形势分析，既要看到在全球经济总体“低增长、高通胀”格
局下中国经济的韧性和亮点，也要看到面临的需求不足尤其是消费低迷、企
业账款清欠不及时、房地产持续调整及地方财政紧平衡等问题，更要看到政
策和改革的空间和潜力依然较大，徙木立信、行胜于言。当前的核心是针对
三大微观主体尽快推出系列改革和政策激发其积极性，从改革看，对于居民
部门推出收入分配和社保改革、 对于企业部门推出产权保护和市场准入改革、
对于地方政府推出财税改革；从政策看，进一步提高地方政府债务限额或者
增发国债转移支付给地方政府，恢复其发展经济的能力，度过新旧动能转换
的第二阶段（新旧动能并行但旧动能仍占主导）并进入第三阶段（新动能占
主导） ，如此则中国经济增速稳定在中速平台依然可期。 
前三季度，我国 GDP 同比增长 4.6%，其中一季度增长 5.0%、二季度增长
4.3%、三季度回升至 4.5%，年内总体呈“U 型”走势。与 2025 年经济增速
逐季放缓不同， 今年三季度已经出现回升； 与2024 年经济增速在四季度才回
升相比，回升时点也提前了一个季度。除了低基数带来的同比改善外，经季
节性调整后的三季度 GDP 环比增长 1.2%，比二季度加快 0.3 个百分点，表
明经济增长的实际动能也有所增强。9 月工业和服务业生产、消费、出口等
多项指标增速加快，投资出现边际企稳迹象，经济运行的积极变化进一步增
多。 
三季度经济回升主要得益于外需持续走强、工业和服务业生产改善，以及宏
观政策加力提效。全球 AI 投资扩张和传统消费品需求改善共同推动出口明显
提速，高技术制造业和现代服务业较快发展，带动生产端持续改善；消费品
以旧换新、 设备更新、 专项债加快发行等政策也发挥了托底作用。9 月工业和
服务业生产进一步加快， 消费有所改善， 投资出现边际企稳迹象，9 月环比增
长 0.07%，结束了此前连续数月环比下降的局面；1-9 月投资累计降幅与 1-8
月持平，未进一步下滑。但总体来看，本轮回升仍主要由外需和生产端驱动，
消费和投资虽然有所好转，内生需求恢复仍然偏弱。 
经济增长的“K 型分化”尚未明显收敛， 而且分化不仅体现在新旧产业之间，
也体现在生产、营收、利润、投资以及居民收入和消费等环节。AI 相关产业
保持较高景气度，但部分传统行业需求不足、产能利用率偏低；一些行业生
产增长难以带来收入和利润同步改善，另一些行业虽然受益于价格上涨、盈
利明显增长，却未相应扩大投资。这反映出供强需弱、内卷式竞争和投资回
报预期偏弱等问题。同时，新动能的就业和收入带动面仍然有限，居民收入
增长相对温和，低于经济增速；消费支出增速更低，消费倾向持续下降，三
季度居民平均消费倾向（人均消费支出/人均可支配收入）为 66.8%，低于去
年同期。其背后既有新兴产业就业吸纳能力和增长收益扩散范围的制约，也
有房地产调整、居民财富效应减弱和收入预期偏谨慎等因素的影响。 
展望四季度，外需韧性、 新动能成长和前期政策效果释放仍将支撑经济增长，

### Page 2

宏观深度 
请务必阅读最后特别声明与免责条款 www.ykzq.com  2 / 10 
但房地产持续调整、企业投资和居民消费偏弱，以及外部环境变化等因素也
将制约回升力度。预计四季度 GDP 同比增长 4.7%左右， 全年增长4.6%左右，
有望实现全年预期目标 ；全年经济增速达到 4.5%，要求四季度最低增速为
4.2%。但考虑到当前经济增长潜力尚未充分释放、物价涨幅偏低、微观主体
预期偏弱， 不能仅满足于目标区间下限， 而应争取推动经济增速向上沿靠拢。
四季度稳增长需要坚持存量政策加快落地与增量政策适时推出相结合， 重点
提高财政资金和政策性金融工具使用效率，促进有效投资和民间投资恢复；
加大对居民收入、就业和消费的直接支持；加快房地产去库存和风险化解。

风险提示：外部冲击超预期、稳增长政策超预期

一、三季度经济增速回升，9 月积极变化进一步增多
三季度经济回升主要有三方面原因。
一是外需持续走强，出口增长明显提速，成为经济回升的重要动力。根据海关数据
测算，按美元计价，三季度出口同比增长28.4%， 比二季度加快8.3 个百分点； 其中9 月
出口同比增长 36.3%，比 8 月加快 11.4 个百分点。出口较快增长，一方面得益于全球AI
基础设施投资扩张和绿色转型带来的需求，集成电路、计算机设备、汽车等产品表现突
出；另一方面，海外传统消费品需求改善，叠加我国制造业较强的产业链配套能力和供
应链竞争力，家电及服装、鞋靴、箱包、玩具等传统消费品出口也明显加快。同时，出
口市场多元化持续推进，增强了外贸韧性。出口需求扩张不仅直接带动外贸增长，也通
过订单增加带动相关制造业生产，对三季度经济回升形成重要支撑。
二是工业生产回升、服务业保持韧性，9 月生产端改善更加明显。三季度第二产业
增加值同比增长 3.4%，比二季度加快 0.4 个百分点；第三产业增长 5.2%，加快 0.1 个百

分点。工业方面，受出口订单增长、高技术制造业和装备制造业较快发展、以及大规模
设备更新政策等因素带动， 规模以上工业增加值同比增速从7 月的 4.5%回升至 8 月的 5.2%
和 9 月的 5.5%。其中，9 月计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业增加值增长 16.4%，专
用设备制造业增长 12.1%，反映出外需扩张和产业升级对工业生产的带动作用。服务业
方面，AI 应用和企业数字化转型带动信息技术、商务服务等生产性服务业较快发展，暑
期旅游和出行需求也对交通运输、住宿餐饮等行业形成支撑。9 月服务业生产指数同比
增长 4.8%，比 8 月份加快 0.7 个百分点。总体看，出口需求与新动能发展共同推动工业
生产回升，现代服务业增长和部分服务消费需求改善则增强了经济增长的韧性。
三是宏观政策持续加力，消费和投资出现一定边际改善。消费方面，6 月底下达第
三批 625 亿元超长期特别国债资金，保障三季度消费品以旧换新政策接续实施；8 月进
一步扩大个人消费贷款、服务业经营主体及中小微企业贷款贴息支持范围和额度，对部

分商品消费和服务消费形成支持。9 月社会消费品零售总额同比增长 1.1%，比 8 月加快
0.7 个百分点； 其中剔除汽车后的消费品零售额同比增长3.9%，比8 月加快 1.4 个百分点。
投资方面，7 月初全年 2000 亿元超长期特别国债设备更新资金全部下达，支持约 1.1 万
个项目； 三季度新增地方政府专项债发行约1.48 万亿元， 明显高于二季度的0.91 万亿元，
加强了重点项目资金保障。9 月投资也出现边际企稳迹象，前三季度固定资产投资累计
同比下降 7.2%，降幅与 1—8 月份持平，9 月环比增长 0.07%，结束了此前连续数月环比
下降的局面。9 月初启动投放总规模 8000 亿元的新型政策性金融工具，将进一步加强项
目资本金保障，其效果有望在四季度逐步显现。
总体来看，三季度经济增速回升、9 月份多项指标改善，反映出外需和新动能持续
发挥重要作用，前期宏观政策也在逐步显效。但本轮回升仍主要由外需和生产端带动，
消费和投资虽然出现一定边际改善，总体仍然偏弱，经济增长向企业经营效益、投资扩

张和居民消费的传导尚不充分。

二、 经济“K 型分化”仍然明显， 企业投资和居民消费改善相
对滞后 
“K 型分化”是今年以来中国经济运行的突出特征，三季度经济增速回升、9 月份
多项指标改善，但这种结构性分化尚未明显收敛。一方面，AI 相关产业、高技术制造业
和现代服务业保持较快增长，新动能持续壮大；另一方面，房地产及部分传统产业仍在
调整，国内需求恢复相对缓慢。更值得关注的是，经济增长的分化不仅存在于不同行业
之间，也体现在生产增长与企业营收、利润、投资以及居民收入、消费之间，生产端的
较快增长尚未形成广泛而顺畅的传导效应。 
首先，新旧动能和工业行业内部分化明显，部分行业产能利用率偏低，供强需弱问
题仍较突出。前三季度， 高技术制造业增加值同比增长14.3%， 比上年同期加快4.7 个百
分点。其中，计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业增加值增长 15.8%，铁路、船舶、航
空航天和其他运输设备制造业增长 12.7%；而受房地产产业链影响，非金属矿物制品业
下降 2.9%。产能利用情况同样存在明显差异。三季度全国规模以上工业产能利用率为
73.1%，比上年同期下降 1.5 个百分点。其中，电子设备制造业产能利用率达到 80.2%，
同比提高 1.2 个百分点；汽车制造业仅为 66.2%，同比下降 7.1 个百分点；非金属矿物制
品业更是低至 61.2%。 
其次， 企业生产增长与经营效益改善、 投资扩张并不同步， 行业间差异尤为明显。1—8
月，规模以上工业企业营业收入同比增长 6.6%，利润总额增长 15.7%，但盈利改善主要
集中在部分行业。其中，电子设备制造业营业收入同比增长 20.1%，利润增长 109.9%，
前三季度投资同比增长 9.6%，受 AI 相关需求和产品价格上涨等因素带动，呈现生产、
盈利和投资共同增长的态势。 相比之下， 汽车制造业1—8 月营业收入同比仅增长 2.9%，
利润下降 16.0%，前三季度投资下降 6.3%，这与终端需求偏弱、市场竞争激烈、产品价
格承压等有关。此外，部分行业营收和利润改善具有较强的价格驱动特征，但在产能利
用不足、未来需求和盈利持续性尚不明朗，以及前期项目投资周期等因素影响下，盈利
改善并未同步转化为投资增长。例如，有色金属冶炼和压延加工业前三季度增加值同比

下降 1.6%，但 1—8 月营业收入增长 18.5%、 利润增长82.9%，主要受到金属价格大幅上
涨影响，9 月该行业 PPI 同比上涨 21.4%，而前三季度投资却下降 5.1%；化学原料和化
学制品制造业 1—8 月利润同比增长 51.0%，但前三季度投资下降 7.5%。 
再次，新动能增长红利向居民就业、收入和消费的扩散有限。AI、高端制造等产业
快速发展，带动相关企业生产经营改善，也对部分高技能岗位需求形成支撑，但尚未形
成广泛的就业和收入带动效应。前三季度，全国城镇调查失业率平均为5.2%，与上年同
期持平； 全国居民人均可支配收入名义同比增长5.3%， 略低于同期名义GDP 增速（5.6%），
居民收入增长未能明显跑赢经济总量增长；居民收入中位数同比增长4.6%，低于人均收
入增速 0.7 个百分点。与此同时，居民人均消费支出名义同比增长 3.6%，比上年同期放
缓 1 个百分点， 明显低于收入增速。 居民收入总体保持增长， 但消费支出增长相对滞后，
经济增长尚未充分转化为居民购买力和消费需求的同步改善。三季度居民平均消费倾向
（人均消费支出/人均可支配收入）为 66.8%，自 2024 年同期以来持续回落。 
最后，增长传导不畅受到多重因素制约。一是部分行业如汽车生产能力较强，但国
内有效需求不足，企业面临较强竞争和盈利压力，生产增长难以充分转化为经营效益，
进而制约投资扩张。二是 AI、高端制造等新兴产业技术和资本密集度较高，产出快速增
长未必带来同比例的就业增加，而房地产、建筑及部分传统服务业调整影响的就业群体
相对广泛，新旧产业之间的就业吸纳能力存在差异。三是新兴产业增长收益更多首先体
现在部分优势企业和高技能劳动者，向产业链中小企业及普通劳动者扩散仍需要时间。
四是房地产持续调整削弱了居民财富效应，叠加就业收入预期偏谨慎以及养老、医疗、
教育等长期支出压力，增加了居民预防性储蓄需求，制约消费意愿改善。 
因此，当前“K 型分化”的深层问题，不仅是新旧动能增长速度不同，更在于生产
增长、企业盈利、投资扩张、居民收入和消费之间尚未形成顺畅的良性循环。缓解结构
性分化，需要在培育新动能的同时，着力改善供需关系和企业盈利预期，扩大增长的就
业和收入带动面，增强经济增长的内生动力。

三、四季度经济展望及政策建议 
（一）四季度经济展望 
前三季度 GDP 同比增长 4.6%， 为完成全年4.5%—5%的经济增长目标奠定了较好基
础。按照上年四季度 GDP 占全年约 27.7%的比重粗略测算，今年四季度 GDP 同比仅需
增长 4.2%，便有望支撑全年增长 4.5%左右；如果四季度保持 4.5%—5%的增长，全年
增速大致在 4.6%—4.7%之间。 
然而， 当前不能仅满足于实现目标区间的下沿， 而应争取推动经济增速向上沿靠拢。
从工业产能利用率偏低、投资和消费需求不足等情况看，经济增长潜力尚未得到充分释
放，实际增速仍可能低于潜在增长水平；前三季度CPI 同比仅上涨 0.9%，明显低于全年
2%左右的预期目标，企业和居民的信心与预期也有待进一步改善。因此，有必要保持宏
观政策力度， 推动经济在实现合理增长的同时， 加快改善供需关系和微观主体经营状况。 
四季度经济保持平稳增长具备一定支撑，但增长动力的持续性仍存在不确定性。预
计四季度 GDP 同比增长 4.7%，全年增长 4.6%左右。一方面，全球 AI 基础设施投资、
绿色转型和传统消费品需求有望继续支撑出口，高技术制造业、装备制造业和现代服务
业保持较快发展，前期出台的财政金融政策包括新型政策性金融工具、地方债务结存限
额以及“六张网”部署安排也将进一步落地显效，为四季度经济增长提供有利条件。另
一方面，这些支撑因素仍较多集中于外需和生产端，对国内需求的带动尚不充分，而且
外需本身也存在波动风险。 全球AI 投资能否延续高增长、 国际贸易摩擦是否加剧， 以及
地缘政治和能源价格波动，都可能影响出口表现。国内方面，房地产市场持续调整、企
业投资意愿不足和居民消费偏弱，仍是制约经济增长的主要因素。同时，部分行业产能
利用率偏低，企业生产增长向盈利和投资的传导不够顺畅。如果四季度出口增速有所回
落，而国内需求又未能明显改善，经济增长仍可能面临下行压力。

（二）政策建议
四季度稳增长需要坚持存量政策加快落地与增量政策适时推出相结合，更加突出扩
大有效需求、改善企业经营和稳定居民预期。
一是加快财政资金和政策性金融工具落地，促进有效投资和民间投资恢复。加快使
用 10 月初安排的 5500 亿元地方政府债务结存限额， 其中3000 亿元一般债务额度重点用
于增强县区财政保障能力，2500 亿元专项债务额度支持四季度有实际资金需求的重点项
目。 加快剩余新增专项债额度发行使用， 以及总规模8000 亿元的新型政策性金融工具投
放，优先支持在建项目、城市更新、公共服务补短板和基础设施存量改造等领域，尽快
形成实物工作量，避免在产能利用率较低的行业重复扩大供给。同时，加快清理拖欠企
业账款，完善民间资本参与重大项目的机制，推动更多具有稳定收益的项目向民间资本
开放，改善企业现金流和投资回报预期。
二是加大居民收入和消费支持力度，推动消费需求更广泛恢复。用好 9 月底下达的

第四批 625 亿元消费品以旧换新资金，加快补贴审核兑付，推动个人消费贷款、服务业
经营主体及中小微企业贷款贴息政策更好落地。在此基础上，可以考虑由中央财政适时
推出更有针对性的增量措施，对中低收入群体和困难家庭给予一次性生活补助，并对养
老、托育、家政、文旅等服务消费给予适度支持，扩大政策受益面。同时，加大对吸纳
就业较多的中小微企业的稳岗和社保补贴力度，支持高校毕业生等重点群体就业，促进
就业、收入和消费形成良性循环。
三是加快房地产去库存和风险化解，稳定住房需求和市场预期。提高已出台的首套
住房商业性个人住房贷款贴息政策实施效率，简化审核和办理流程，支持符合条件的刚
性住房需求，并因城施策进一步支持合理改善性住房需求。加快推动存量商品房收购用
作保障性住房，完善收购定价、租金收益平衡和风险分担机制，对符合条件的收储项目
考虑给予适度中央财政贴息或风险补偿，提高专项债和保障性住房再贷款的实际使用效
率。同时，根据不同城市库存和人口变化情况，合理控制新增住宅用地供应，加快盘活
闲置土地，继续做好保交房和房地产企业债务风险化解，促进房地产市场逐步恢复供需

平衡。
四是尽快研究进一步提高地方政府债务限额或者增发国债转移支付给地方政府，以
弥补房地产调整带来的财力缺口，支持地方化债、收储和清欠等。当前三大微观主体积
极性的核心是地方政府积极性和财力不足，要以恢复地方政府财力为契机带动企业账款
清欠的解决以及提高居民公共服务等问题。

股票投资评级标准 
报告发布日后的 12 个月内公司股价的涨跌幅度相对同期沪深 300 指数的涨跌幅为基准，投资建议的评级标准为： 
买入：相对大盘涨幅大于 10%； 
增持：相对大盘涨幅在 5%~10%之间； 
持有：相对大盘涨幅在-5%~5%之间； 
减持：相对大盘涨幅小于-5%。

行业投资评级标准 
报告发布日后的 12 个月内行业股票指数的涨跌幅度相对同期沪深 300 指数的涨跌幅为基准，投资建议的评级标准为： 
增持：我们预计未来报告期内，行业整体回报高于基准指数 5%以上； 
中性：我们预计未来报告期内，行业整体回报介于基准指数-5%与 5%之间； 
减持：我们预计未来报告期内，行业整体回报低于基准指数 5%以下。

• 非核心消費板塊獲利前景近
期蒙上陰霾。首先，郵輪業
雖受惠於旅遊需求韌性而上
調 2026 財年預測，但高利
率與高油價恐使後市持續承
壓；其次，運動鞋服產業受
到大中華區市場衰退與新興
小品牌崛起雙重打擊，面臨
營收與獲利雙雙下滑的困境

顯現。再者，在即將迎來的

滯。

美3Q26財報季將陸續揭曉 ，目前市場共識預期標
普500指數各產業板塊第三季的獲利成長存在較大
差異，其中資訊科技和能源板塊將推動指數整體獲
利成長，預期非核心消費板塊整體每股盈餘將不會
成長。另外，零星個股捎來截至 8月底的季度業績
也顯示好壞參半的結果。
《郵輪旅遊需求仍具韌性，燃油成本仍是潛在風險》
嘉年華郵輪 (CCL)3Q26財年(截至8月底)營收年增
3.5%，每股盈餘持平 ，淨收益率超預期 ，主要貢
獻來自船上及其它收入，然因燃料成本大增36%，
完全抵銷了營運效率與成本控制的效益 ，導致調整

後EBITDA持平。管理層上調2026年全年獲利展望 ，
且公司表示 2027年訂位量及訂位價格均創下歷史
新高，目前已售出約半數 2027年艙位 ，顯示旅遊
需求仍具韌性。但市場對其未來半年的獲利預期偏

《運動鞋服Nike轉型成效慢，獲利展望不佳》
Nike1Q27財年(2026/6~2026/8)營收與每股盈餘
皆衰退。運動休閒系列、Jordan品牌和大中華區市
場下滑 ，成為主要逆風 。管理層預期 2027財年營

畫，但效益明顯無法在短期內看到 。其中一個原因
是Nike的市場主導地位已不如前，小型品牌如今已

具備規模化營運能力，當消費者行為已發生結構性
轉變時(品牌忠誠度降低與多品牌嘗試 )，如何收復
市佔率就成為一項艱巨且持久的挑戰。
《2026 美年底消費總額預期創新高 ，逾半增長靠
「通膨」，電商與生成式AI成雙引擎》
近期已有多家研調機構針對美國2026年年底消費
旺季出具預測報告。整體來看，各大機構預測的銷
售總額皆創下歷史新高 ，但其背後的共同隱憂是
「通膨」，直言超過一半的名目增長率是由物價上
漲驅動。這使得消費者普遍展現「價格敏感、主動
尋找折扣、切換品牌以省錢」的價值導向行為。電
商增速(網購)依然大幅碾壓實體零售。所有機構也
都點出生成式AI在2026年成為主流購物工具。
資料來源：Mastercard， 2026/9/23，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/13

機構預測2026年底消費旺季
各家機構重點分述如下
Mastercard ： 預 期 假日消費成長近半因物價上漲 ，
今年主因能源與AI建設成本增加，去年則受關稅影
響。鑑於柴油價格持續高企可能推高零售商的運輸
成本，並對年底的整體消費價格構成上行壓力，因
此值得密切關注。高收入家庭因財富成長、增加自
由支配支出，成為近年消費成長的重要引領者， 然
而，受益於勞動市場穩定、薪資成長與健康資產負
債表，各收入群體亦持續穩定貢獻，消費仍由廣大
家庭共同支撐。
Adobe：預計今年假期季節將推出極具競爭力的折

扣，雖然消費者歷來都熱衷於利用節日促銷活動購
買禮物或添加最新電子產品，但許多人現在更傾向
於利用折扣囤積生活必需品。另，今年假期季節美
國零售網站的人工智慧流 量 將年增130%。預計感
恩節的增幅最大(年增159%)，其次是黑色星期五(
年成長 95%)和網路星期一(年增88%)。
德勤(Deloitte) ： 消費者仍然重視為親朋好友營造
特別的節日氛圍，同時也在精打細算地消費。為了
更好地利用每一分錢，觀察到各個收入階層的消費
者都在追求性價比，例如在不同品牌和零售商之間
切換，以及利用促銷活動來控制支出。
